PRG bank 7

the fixed bank: vectors, bank switching, the far-call trampoline, the inline-argument helpers · $C000-$FFFF

Documented
100.00%
Bytes
16,384 / 16,384
Regions
658
File offsets
0x1C010-0x2000F

Why this bank sits where it does

UxROM wires the last bank to $C000-$FFFF permanently, and this is the last of the eight. Confirmed rather than assumed: the three interrupt vectors in this bank's own tail point at $DC75, $FEDE and $DCF3, and all three addresses decode as sensible handlers when this bank's bytes are placed at $C000. The seven fixed-address bank-switch routines at $D714-$D76E and the generic one at $D705 are also here, as they must be - code that switches the $8000 window cannot be running in the $8000 window. The boot-time bank check at $FF23-$FF43 selects each of the other seven banks in turn and confirms it answers with its own number, so the reset path itself asserts that this bank stays put while the others move.

This is the fixed bank. UxROM wires the last PRG bank to $C000-$FFFF and there is no register that can move it, so these bytes are visible to the CPU at every moment the console is running. That is why the interrupt vectors, the bank-switching routines and the far-call trampoline are all here: none of them could work from a window that is about to be switched out from under them.

Layout

codedatagraphicspaddingpalettepointer_tabletextvectorsunclaimed

Unclaimed

None - every byte in this span is claimed by a region.

BANK 7 JUMP TABLE, slot $C000: reset code confirmed

bank7.code_C000 · file 0x1C010-0x1C012 · 3 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $C000-$C002

`JMP $FEDE`: The RESET vector: interrupts off, stack, three vblanks waited, memory cleared, the mapper checked, then the game (`$FEDE`).

$C000  4C DE FE                                         |L..|

BANK 7 JUMP TABLE, slot $C003: bank switch to A code confirmed

bank7.code_C003 · file 0x1C013-0x1C015 · 3 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $C003-$C005

`JMP $D705`: Switch to bank A (`$04` shadow, `LDA/STA $FFF2,Y`). Y is preserved.

$C003  4C 05 D7                                         |L..|

BANK 7 JUMP TABLE, slot $C006: far call code confirmed

bank7.code_C006 · file 0x1C016-0x1C018 · 3 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $C006-$C008

`JMP $D76F`: The far-call trampoline: bank, target - 1 (low, high) follow in the instruction stream.

Metasprite pointers, low bytes pointer_table confirmed

bank7.tbl_C28D · file 0x1C29D-0x1C328 · 140 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $C28D-$C318

The low half of the split pointer table an object's $0548,X indexes. The high half begins at $C319, which is why this half measures 140 - and the halves OVERLAP by that one byte, because $C28D + 140 is $C319 and high[0] is never read. See tools/tgl/metasprite.py. Extent: 140 bytes, bounded by the base at $C319 read from $CC3C.

bank7.tbl_C319 · file 0x1C329-0x1C3B5 · 141 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $C319-$C3A5

The high half of the split metasprite table: $CC3C LDA $C319,Y with Y = the object's $0548 id. 141 entries, ending exactly where the first metasprite record begins at $C3A6 - the structural bound; no later read bounds it, which is why tools/find-tables.py claimed only the low half at $C28D. Metasprites: 115 records, 53 offset tables graphics confirmed

bank7.sprite_C3A6 · file 0x1C3B6-0x1CBF7 · 2,114 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $C3A6-$CBE7

$C3A6-$CBE7. 115 metasprite records and 53 (x, y) offset tables, interleaved. A record is [count][offsets lo][offsets hi][tiles][attributes], named by the split pointer table at $C28D (low) and $C319 (high) and drawn by $CC46. Bit 7 of the count means draw the whole thing twice with bit 6 of the attribute flipped - a free mirror image. Tile numbers are RELATIVE, added to the object's own base tile at $87, and $FF skips a sprite. The attribute array is run-length coded: $CE23 advances it only when bit 3 of the byte is clear, which is why a record's length does not follow from its count. Offset tables are shared - 118 metasprites use 57 of them - so records and tables interleave and this region is a contiguous run of covered bytes rather than one record. Generated by tools/parse-metasprites.py; the format is in tools/tgl/metasprite.py.

$C3A6  E8 F0 F0 F0 F8 F0 00 F0 08 F0 10 F0 E8 00 F0 00  |................|
$C3B6  F8 00 00 00 08 00 10 00 E8 10 F0 10 F8 10 00 10  |................|
$C3C6  08 10 10 10 F0 F0 F8 F0 00 F0 08 F0 F0 00 F8 00  |................|
$C3D6  00 00 08 00 F8 F0 00 F0 F8 00 00 00 F4 F8 FC F8  |................|
$C3E6  04 F8 F0 E8 F8 E8 00 E8 08 E8 F0 F8 F8 F8 00 F8  |................|
$C3F6  08 F8 F0 08 F8 08 00 08 08 08 EC F8 F4 F8 FC F8  |................|
$C406  04 F8 0C F8 E8 F8 F0 F8 F8 F8 00 F8 08 F8 10 F8  |................|
$C416  FC F0 FC 00 F8 D8 00 D8 F8 E8 00 E8 F8 F8 00 F8  |................|

First 128 of 2,114 bytes. The region's SHA-1 ae02a461472293d4b4f707b368d2327f3f06045e covers all of them.

Per-type value copied to $0620 data confirmed

bank7.tbl_D1A9 · file 0x1D1B9-0x1D238 · 128 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $D1A9-$D228

$CFA0 copies it into the slot with LDA table,Y where Y = type AND $7F - four reads six bytes apart, which is also what bounds the tables. Destination $0620: DRAWING FLAGS. The draw path $CBE8 tests them bit by bit - bit 0 calls $CC88 and bit 1 $CCEB before drawing, bit 2 flips the attribute when $05D8 is non-negative, bit 3 is tested at $CC1F - so the per-type value says how the object is drawn. What each extra step does is not yet read. 128 bytes, ending at the code at $D229.

2x2 tile groups for $C054 data confirmed

bank7.tbl_D8E1 · file 0x1D8F1-0x1D93C · 76 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $D8E1-$D92C

$D865 (entry $C054) draws a 2x2 block with its attribute. When A AND 3 is non-zero it reads the four tiles from $D8E0 + A ... $D8E3 + A (LDA $D8E0,Y with Y = A); when it is zero it uses A, A+1 ... directly. So A itself is the byte offset into this table and any A with a low bit set names a group; the groups can overlap. The extent is structural - the last byte of $D865's code is at $D8E0 and $D92D is code - and which groups are actually used depends on callers not enumerated here.

D-pad to direction ($32) data confirmed

bank7.tbl_DB22 · file 0x1DB32-0x1DB41 · 16 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $DB22-$DB31

$DB15: LDA $F7 / ORA $F8 / AND #$0F / TAY / LDA $DB22,Y / STA $32 - the held d-pad of either controller (Up 8, Down 4, Left 2, Right 1) to a direction code 0-8. Values: none 4, R 7, L 1, L+R 4, D 3, D+R 6, D+L 0, U 5, U+R 8, U+L 2 - a 3 x 3 grid read row by row, 4 the centre, opposite presses cancelling. Runs every frame through $DAE1 ($C02D), for the demo and for a player alike.

$DB22  04 07 01 04 03 06 00 03 05 08 02 05 04 07 01 04  |................|

Demo: the button patterns the attract mode holds data confirmed

bank7.tbl_DB32 · file 0x1DB42-0x1DB61 · 32 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $DB32-$DB51

$DAE1 ($C02D, called by the player's handler, bank 3 $8CF3): while $30 bit 7 (the demo) is set, every time the hold timer $F2 runs out it takes $F2 = random AND $0F + $18 (24-40 frames, $F9D1), steps $F4 = ($F4 AND $1F) + 1 and loads $F3 = $DB32,Y with Y = $F4; every frame it writes $F3 into $F7/$F8 (held) and $F5/$F6 (new), replacing whatever the controllers said. So the demo is NOT a recording of a real game: it cycles through these patterns, starting at a random one ($F4 = random AND $1F, bank 0 $8170), each held for a random time. Y runs 1-32, so $DB32 itself (Y = 0) is never read and the real patterns are $DB33-$DB51, 31 of them, every one holding A and B (fire and the weapon) plus a direction or none. Y = 32 reads $DB52 - the first byte of the tile decoder, the opcode $A5 - which as buttons is A + Select + Down + Right; Select does nothing in the demo because $F493 skips the pause/status check when $30 bit 7 is set. The extent stays at the find-tables bound; the table proper is $DB33-$DB51.

$DB32  C2 C6 C0 CA C6 C1 C2 C1 C4 C5 C8 C6 C0 C9 C5 C2  |................|
$DB42  C4 C0 C5 C2 CA C4 C0 C1 C0 C5 C1 CA C9 C0 C5 C9  |................|

bank7.code_DB5A · file 0x1DB6A-0x1DB74 · 11 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $DB5A-$DB64

`$0B`: bit 0 = running; `LSR`: running goes on at `$DB65`; the next bit (value 2 = just armed) loads the header (`$DC5A`); 0 = idle, return. **Other bits while running: bit 7 = a run section (`[value][count]` pairs), bit 6 = mirror mode (bits reversed), bit 5 = a run being emitted.**

NMI, PPU QUEUE, PALETTES AND THE SCORE BONUS (bank 7 `$DC75-$DFAE`): the DMC IRQ (the IRQ vector) code confirmed

bank7.code_DCF3 · file 0x1DD03-0x1DD30 · 46 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $DCF3-$DD20

`$4015` := `$0F` (acknowledge). **If sprite 0 is already hit when the IRQ arrives (PPUSTATUS bit 6), it is late: the DMC rate index `$05` is incremented (kept at `$89` at most) and the new rate written to DMC_FREQ, without a split.** Otherwise it spins on PPUSTATUS until the hit (`$DD21` makes the split, then the rate `$05` is written) or until the frame counter `$2B` changes (it gives up). The rate thus converges so that the IRQ arrives just before the split line.

$DCF3  48 A9 0F 8D 15 40 A5 2B 2C 02 20 70 15 2C 02 20  |H....@.+,. p.,. |
$DD03  70 06 C5 2B F0 F7 D0 08 20 21 DD A5 05 8D 10 40  |p..+.... !.....@|
$DD13  68 40 E6 05 A5 05 C9 8A D0 F1 C6 05 D0 ED        |h@............|

Far-call argument at $DF9E data confirmed

bank7.inline_DF9E · file 0x1DFAE-0x1DFB0 · 3 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $DF9E-$DFA0

Three bytes of far-call argument following `JSR $D76F` at bank 7 $DF9B - bank number, then the target address minus one, little-endian. They select **bank 6 $FCE7**. The stored word is $FCE6 rather than $FCE7 because $D790 pushes these two bytes as a fake return address and lets RTS add the one back; that is not an inference, it is what the four instructions at $D799-$D7A4 do. A far call is the one reference in this ROM that no grep for an operand will ever surface, so the target is recorded here where a reader will find it.

$DF9E  06 E6 FC                                         |...|

KILL SCORE (slot $C02A) code confirmed

bank7.code_DFB1 · file 0x1DFC1-0x1DFDE · 30 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $DFB1-$DFCE

Called by the explosion handler when an enemy dies (X = the explosion, `$0740,X` = the enemy's type). The score class is `$E00D[type]`; when its bit 7 is set (types `$27` and `$2C`) the object's own `$07D0,X` is the class (the bullet kinds' `$A207` values, the Clawbot arms' `$0B`). Nothing in a demo (`$30` bit 7). Otherwise `JSR $DFCF` adds the points, the kill counter `$40` is incremented and the bonus check `$DF3D` runs. RUN: all 127 types: the points added equal the tables (claimed by `parse-enemies.py`: `$E00D` the class per type, `$E08D` the digits). In points, by class: 0, 10, 60, 100, 170, 200, 300, 500, 800, 1000, 2000, 4000, 8000, 10000, 15000, 20000, 30000, 40000. Per type: the player-side and scenery types 1-10, `$28 $29 $30-$35 $77-$79` score nothing; `$0B $0C` 10; `$0D-$11 $1C $2B $62 $6F` 60; `$1B $44 $52 $5F $63` 100; ordinary enemies 170-1000; bosses: Zibzub, Optomon `$2D-$2F`, `$47` and `$4A / $4B` 10,000; `$18 $24 $25 $26 $48` 20,000; `$19 $49` 30,000; `$41 $46` 15,000; `$40 $45 $4D` 8,000; `$4C` 4,000; It (`$4F`) 40,000. The bullet kinds' `$07D0` (`$A207`: 3 5 8 7 8 6 6 6) and the Clawbot arms' `$0B` are therefore SCORE CLASSES - 100 to 800 points a bullet, 4,000 an arm.

$DFB1  BC 40 07 B9 0D E0 10 03 BD D0 07 85 10 A5 30 29  |.@............0)|
$DFC1  80 D0 48 A5 10 20 CF DF E6 40 20 3D DF 60        |..H.. ...@ =.`|

Add points to the score code confirmed

bank7.code_DFCF · file 0x1DFDF-0x1E01C · 62 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $DFCF-$E00C

Class A: Y := 6 x A + 6 into the digit table `$E08D` (six decimal digits a class, most significant first; the score is kept in tens, so the real value is the number x 10). `$017C` bit 7 set (the score has overflowed) returns. The digits are added from the last with a carry (a digit of 10 or more has 10 subtracted). A carry out of the top digit sets the score to 999999 (all 9s), runs the bonus award `$DF8C` once and sets `$017C` := `$FF`.

$DFCF  85 10 0A 18 65 10 0A 69 06 A8 86 10 AD 7C 01 30  |....e..i.....|.0|
$DFDF  EE A2 05 18 B9 8C E0 7D 70 01 C9 0A 90 02 E9 0A  |.......}p.......|
$DFEF  9D 70 01 88 CA 10 ED 90 12 A9 09 A0 05 99 70 01  |.p............p.|
$DFFF  88 10 FA 20 8C DF A9 FF 8D 7C 01 A6 10 60        |... .....|...`|

Score class, by type data confirmed

bank7.tbl_E00D · file 0x1E01D-0x1E09C · 128 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E00D-$E08C

$DFB1: LDY $0740,X (the dead object's type, saved by $D32A) / LDA $E00D,Y; negative means per object, from $07D0,X. $DFCF turns the class into six decimal digits at $E08C + 6 x class + 1 and adds them into the score at $0170-$0175, capping at 999999. Classes 0-17 occur, plus $FF. 128 entries: type 127's is the byte at $E08C, which is also the never-read first byte of the digit rows (class 0's digits start at $E08D) - the overlap trick the split tables use. Values: class 0 = 0, class 1 = 1, class 2 = 6, class 3 = 10, class 4 = 17, class 5 = 20, class 6 = 30, class 7 = 50, class 8 = 80, class 9 = 100, class 10 = 200, class 11 = 400, class 12 = 800, class 13 = 1000, class 14 = 1500, class 15 = 2000, class 16 = 3000, class 17 = 4000.

bank7.code_E0F9 · file 0x1E109-0x1E171 · 105 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E0F9-$E161

A run of 105 bytes the code trace reached by following control flow, in bank 7 at $E0F9-$E161. **This region claims the extent and nothing else.** What the code does has not been established; the evidence is that every byte here is part of an instruction on some path the trace could follow from the interrupt vectors and the banks' entry tables, and that the trace met no illegal opcode anywhere. Generated by tools/gen-code-regions.py from tools/trace-code.py - see that tool's --assumptions for what the reachability rests on. To say more about any part of this stretch, claim it in a hand-authored region file; the generator subtracts what is already claimed before it emits, so the hand-written region wins.

"AREA" at $E162 text confirmed

bank7.inline_E162 · file 0x1E172-0x1E178 · 7 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E162-$E168

A queued PPU write item, written inline after `JSR $D576` at bank 7 $E15F. Its shape is [count][ppu_hi][ppu_lo][count characters]: the destination $2762 is in the block itself. The block is 7 bytes and the caller resumes immediately after it; the length comes from the first byte by the rule in $D591 - (byte & $7F) + 3 below $C0, 9 at or above - and $D5A5 advances the routine's own pointer by exactly that. It reads "AREA". Characters are ASCII for the letters and space. NOT ESTABLISHED: the glyphs for codes outside A-Z and space, which only the font can settle - and no font has been found yet, because CHR is RAM on this cartridge.

HUD: the AREA, X and Y line code confirmed

bank7.code_E169 · file 0x1E179-0x1E1B2 · 58 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E169-$E1A2

Row 27 of the HUD (PPU $2766-$2770; the word AREA is the inline string at $E162). Prints the area number - $50 in the labyrinth, $51 when $30 bit 1 is set (a TGL game) - through the 3-digit printer $E1D3, then the map column $52 at $276B and the row $53 at $276F, each with the letter X or Y ($58, $59) written over the hundreds cell of the printed number (so a value of 255 shows as X55). It is entered by FALLING THROUGH from $E15F, which queues the label with the inline-string call $D576; $E15F is called when a room or stage is set up (for example $EB1D). RUN with several values: row 27 read '..AREA..9..X.3.Y17..', '..AREA.10..X55.Y.0..' and, in a TGL game, the corridor number in the area slot.

bank7.code_E1D3 · file 0x1E1E3-0x1E222 · 64 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E1D3-$E212

In: A = the value, $19:$18 = the PPU address. Reserves 6 bytes of the PPU queue ($D6D3 waits for room, $D6E0 reserves), splits A through $E1A3, and builds [3][hi][lo][hundreds][tens][ones] at $0100 + the cursor: digits are $30 + the value, and a zero hundreds or tens cell becomes $20 (blank). It does NOT commit the entry - the callers do ($D6F7). Call sites: the X and Y numbers ($E187, $E198), the shared tail $E1CD used by the area number and the shots-left number, and the life meter's number ($E36C).

Chip tier, then point the number printer at the chip counter code confirmed

bank7.code_E213 · file 0x1E223-0x1E25A · 56 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E213-$E24A

Slot $C05A; also called from $FD0B after the weapon cursor moves. Copies the chips $4C/$4D to $16/$17 and sets the main gun's tier $3A: 0 under 100, 1 for 100-199, 2 for 200-2,047 and 3 from 2,048 (by the high byte $17: 8 or more means 2,048 and over, tier 3; any other non-zero high byte, 256-2,047, tier 2). Then sets $18/$19 = PPU $272F (row 25, after the word CHIP) and FALLS INTO the number printer at $E24B, so every change of the chip total redraws the counter and re-derives the tier in one call. RUN with 15 values: tier 0, 0, 0, 0, 1, 1, 2, 2, 2, 3 ... at 7, 85, 99, 100, 199, 200, 1185, 2047, 2048.

bank7.code_E24B · file 0x1E25B-0x1E31B · 193 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E24B-$E30B

Slot $C04E. In: $17:$16 = the value, $19:$18 = the PPU address. Reserves a 7-byte queue entry [4][hi][lo] + 4 cells (initially '0', '0', ' ', ' ') and builds the digits by repeated subtraction: thousands (up to ten subtractions of 1,000: if all ten succeed, the value is 10,000 or more, the digit cells are set to 9 and the routine ends - so everything from 10,000 up shows 9999), hundreds (100), tens (10) and ones, blanking leading zeros: the digits are left-justified by shifting the write position (INX) when a leading zero is dropped, and the unused cells at the right stay blank (a one-digit value sits one cell in: ' 7 '). The same routine prints the shop's prices above the items (bank 2 $851B). RUN on the chip counter with 0, 7, 85, 99, 100, 199, 200, 1185, 2047, 2048, 9999, 10000, 12345, 40000, 65535: the row read '.0..', '.7..', '.85.', '.99.', '.100', '.199', '.200', '1185', '2047', '2048', '9999' and 9999 for everything larger.

$E25B  A5 19 9D 01 01 A5 18 9D 02 01 A9 30 9D 03 01 9D  |...........0....|
$E26B  04 01 A9 20 9D 05 01 9D 06 01 A9 01 85 10 38 A0  |... ..........8.|
$E27B  0A A5 16 E9 E8 85 16 A5 17 E9 03 85 17 90 13 FE  |................|
$E28B  03 01 88 D0 EC A9 39 9D 03 01 9D 05 01 9D 06 01  |......9.........|
$E29B  D0 67 A5 16 69 E8 85 16 A5 17 69 03 85 17 BD 03  |.g..i.....i.....|
$E2AB  01 C9 30 D0 08 C6 10 A9 20 9D 03 01 38 A5 16 E9  |..0..... ...8...|
$E2BB  64 85 16 A5 17 E9 00 85 17 90 05 FE 04 01 B0 ED  |d...............|

First 128 of 193 bytes. The region's SHA-1 26302cd9c83418b5a6751fd9bab4caf4c621d873 covers all of them.

HUD: the life meter - step the shown value, redraw only what changed code confirmed

bank7.code_E30C · file 0x1E31C-0x1E408 · 237 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E30C-$E3F8

Called first thing every frame by the HUD routine $E0F9. $49 is the SHOWN life, $48 the real one; when they differ the shown value moves one unit toward it per frame (decrease when $49 >= $48, step -1; else +1), so the bar drains and fills visibly. The bar is made of 8-pixel tiles: the tile count is ((value - 1) / 8) + 1 ($15 old, $17 new) and the fill of the last tile is (value - 1) AND 7 ($16). Within one tile only the partial tile changes (the entry at $E3CC); when the value crosses a tile boundary it queues (a) the number and the life icon: the tile COUNT, not the life, as up to three digits at PPU $2740 with tile $23 written in the cell before the first digit ($E357-$E37C); (b) the new last tile as tile $FF, the special partial tile, at bar position $17 (PPU $2742 + position, through $E406); (c) the old last tile, now $22 (empty) when draining or $21 (full) when filling. If the queue lacks room it gives up and the shown value stays, so the redraw is retried next frame. $E3CC queues 5 bytes for PPU $1FF9 - plane 1, rows 1-5 of tile $FF in pattern table 1 - each the fill mask for the last tile ($E3F9[fraction]: 80 C0 E0 F0 F8 FC FE FF) ANDed with the bar's shape for that row ($E401: EE FE FF FF EE, which rounds the ends). So tile $FF's pixels are rewritten every step: the bar's partial segment is drawn by editing CHR RAM. Ends by storing the new value in $49. RUN on the corridor savestate: from 160 down to 32 and back up; row 26 read 'icon, 4, 21 21 21 FF 22 ...' at 32 and 5 at 33-37, and tile $FF's five bytes went ee fe ff ff ee (full) -> ec fc fc fc ec -> e8 f8 f8 f8 e8 -> e0 f0 f0 f0 e0 -> 80 80 80 80 80 as the value fell one pixel at a time.

$E30C  A5 49 85 15 F0 0B 38 E9 01 A8 4A 4A 4A 85 15 E6  |.I....8...JJJ...|
$E31C  15 A5 49 C5 48 F0 33 08 A9 FF B0 02 A9 01 18 65  |..I.H.3........e|
$E32C  49 85 14 85 17 85 16 F0 10 38 E9 01 A8 4A 4A 4A  |I........8...JJJ|
$E33C  85 17 E6 17 98 29 07 85 16 A5 17 C5 15 D0 03 4C  |.....).........L|
$E34C  BE E3 A9 16 20 C0 D6 90 02 28 60 A9 27 85 19 A9  |.... ....(`.'...|
$E35C  40 85 18 A5 14 F0 09 38 E9 01 4A 4A 4A 18 69 01  |@......8..JJJ.i.|
$E36C  20 D3 E1 B9 04 01 C9 20 D0 01 C8 A9 23 99 03 01  | ...... ....#...|
$E37C  20 F7 D6 A9 08 20 E0 D6 A8 28 B0 05 A9 21 48 D0  | .... ...(...!H.|

First 128 of 237 bytes. The region's SHA-1 274e2485dce1a3258092c19fc4e06a48fcbd9f37 covers all of them.

Meter fill masks data confirmed

bank7.tbl_E3F9 · file 0x1E409-0x1E410 · 8 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E3F9-$E400

Eight bytes, $80 $C0 $E0 $F0 $F8 $FC $FE $FF - a bar filling one pixel at a time from the left. Read by LDA $E3F9,X at $E3DD with X from $16. Bounded at both ends: the reader's operand is this address, and the next table begins at $E401, which is $E3F9 + 8. The values are their own argument - eight successive left-aligned runs is a progress bar and nothing else.

$E3F9  80 C0 E0 F0 F8 FC FE FF                          |........|

Meter row masks data confirmed

bank7.tbl_E401 · file 0x1E411-0x1E415 · 5 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E401-$E405

Five bytes, $EE $FE $FF $FF $EE, ANDed with the fill mask at $E3E6 and written to the queue. Bounded by CPX #$05 at $E3EE - a declared count, which per playbook section 9 beats a boundary argument - and the byte after, $E406, is a traced instruction start. WHAT THIS PAIR DOES, and it is the only confirmed CHR write in the ROM so far: $E3CC builds a queue entry of 5 bytes at PPU $1FF9. That address is inside pattern table 1, in tile $FF, not in a nametable - so this routine redraws part of a CHAR TILE every time the meter moves. It is the per-frame status-meter trick playbook section 9 describes finding from the other direction, as three bytes of a pattern table that differed from a savestate.

$E401  EE FE FF FF EE                                   |.....|

Meter: queue one bar tile code confirmed

bank7.code_E406 · file 0x1E416-0x1E433 · 30 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E406-$E423

In: A = bar position (1-24), Y = the queue cursor. Returns carry set and does nothing for 0 or 25 and over; otherwise writes [1][$27][$42 + position] at the cursor, leaves the data byte for the caller and advances Y by 4 (PPU $2743 for position 1, the first bar tile after the number). Called twice by the life meter ($E392, $E3A7) and once by $E424 ($E43A), which the score-bonus code reaches (bank 7 $DFAE) to draw the bar's new empty tile when the maximum life grows.

Life meter: draw the new empty end tile after max life grows code confirmed

bank7.code_E424 · file 0x1E434-0x1E454 · 33 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E424-$E444

Entered only by JMP from the score bonus ($DFAE, after +8 max life). In: A = the new maximum life. A / 8 is the bar position (1-24); 0 or 25 and over does nothing (so a maximum above 191 draws no tile: the bar is 24 tiles wide). Otherwise it waits for 4 bytes of queue room, reserves them, builds the entry through $E406 and writes tile $22 (empty) into it: [1][$27][$42 + position][$22], committed with JMP $D6F7. RUN: A = 8, 100, 160 gave positions 1, 12, 20 (PPU $2743, $274E, $2756); 0, 7, 200, 248, 255 queued nothing.

Clear a whole nametable: entry with A = page, tile $20 code confirmed

bank7.code_E445 · file 0x1E455-0x1E462 · 14 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E445-$E452

Entry from slot $C018 (callers: bank 0 $8178 $817D $8227 $8317 $8377, bank 3 $80A5 $82B7, and the final-score screen at $F67E). Clear rows of a nametable: the row loop (also the second entry, slot $C084) code confirmed

bank7.code_E453 · file 0x1E463-0x1E4A7 · 69 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E453-$E497

Second entry from slot $C084 (bank 3 $8B7B; bank 5 $84C0, the status screen's clear). In: $15 = the nametable's high byte, $14 = fill tile, $17 = first row, $16 = end row (exclusive). The row's PPU address is the pair table at $EAE2 (one entry per 64 bytes: $2000, $2040 ... $23C0) indexed by row AND $FE, ORed with $15, +$20 for an odd row; each row is one 32-tile fill run queued by $D5F8 (the queue entry [$20][hi][lo] + 32 copies of $14), the address then stepping +$20 per row. When the row passes 29 the loop WRAPS to row 0 of the same nametable through its $2800/$2C00 alias ($18 = 0, $19 = ($19 EOR 8) AND $2C) and goes on until the end row. RUN: page $24, rows 28 -> 24 cleared rows 28, 29 and 0-23 of nametable 1 and nothing else; page $20, rows 0 -> 20 and 5 -> 9 cleared exactly those rows. The queue holds only a few rows, so the loop waits (in $D5F8's space check) for the NMI to drain it: 15 frames for a whole nametable.

Weapon level: the two-bit level of weapon A + 1 code confirmed

In: A = weapon index 0-11 (weapon number - 1). The levels are packed four to a byte in $04AC-$04AE, two bits each; the routine doubles A, uses the bit-address helper $EFC7 only for its byte index (Y = A / 4), shifts the byte right (A AND 3) times by two and masks with 3. Callers: $FD82 (the weapon cursor, to get the selected weapon's level into $3D) and, through slot $C075, the status screen (bank 5 $85BD). Pause: Start (or Select) pressed code confirmed

bank7.code_E4B3 · file 0x1E4C3-0x1E4EC · 42 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E4B3-$E4DC

JMP target from the pad routine $F493. A new Start press ($F5 bit 4): plays sound $46, then every frame ($E4DD) waits for a NEW Start or Select; Select (exactly $20) goes to the status screen at $E4EE, Start plays sound $47 and then waits 30 more frames (Y = $1E, one $E4DD each) before returning. $E4E3: no Start, so a new Select ($F5 bit 5) plays $46 and falls into $E4EE; anything else returns at once. bank7.code_E4EE · file 0x1E4FE-0x1E531 · 52 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E4EE-$E521

Plays sound $3E, saves $30 and the live bank on the stack, clears $30 bit 4 (the NMI stops moving the split marker), switches to bank 5 and calls $801B (-> $84CB, open: draws the map and icons), then each frame $E4DD and $8021 (-> $8937, per-frame work) until a NEW Start or Select ($F5 AND $30 non-zero); $801E (-> $8711) closes it, bank 6 is selected and $8000 (-> $81FB, one pass of the object loop; purpose a LEAD - probably to redraw the sprites the screen cleared) is called, the bank and $30 are restored and it jumps to $E4CB: sound $47 and the 30-frame wait of the pause code. Scroll step: move the screen by the speed code confirmed

bank7.code_E530 · file 0x1E540-0x1E56F · 48 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E530-$E55F

$B6 (fraction) -= $B8; the borrow is turned into the carry by ROL / EOR #1 / ROR (A is unchanged), and $B7 = $B9 + that carry: the whole pixels moved this frame. Then $A6 -= $B7; no change returns; no borrow commits ($E522); a borrow means $A6 wrapped below 0, so $A7 (the nametable bit) flips and $A6 - $10 is stored ($FD becomes $ED: the vertical scroll wraps at 240, not 256) before committing. Called by $E66A (the stage step, the launch flight loop $F33E and the stage-clear flight $F528 all go through it) and by the priming loop at $E5D1.

bank7.code_E560 · file 0x1E570-0x1E5CC · 93 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E560-$E5BC

Called by the corridor loader ($EFA3) and the launch flight ($F2F0) with $19:$18 pointing at a bank 4 screen-data block. bank7.code_E5BD · file 0x1E5CD-0x1E5FA · 46 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E5BD-$E5EA

Loads the attribute patterns ($EE09, $0199-$01A0), waits for the decompressor, then repeats: save the speed, set it to $1000 (16 px a frame, one row per pass), one scroll step ($E530), restore the speed, run the row accounting at $E69B (which builds a row because $AF starts at $0F), wait for queue room ($E5EB); it ends when the row index $AE is back at $0D with the half flag $AB clear, i.e. after a whole cycle of 15 rows (240 pixels). Wait until the PPU queue has room and no row is half-sent code confirmed

bank7.code_E5EB · file 0x1E5FB-0x1E614 · 26 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E5EB-$E604

Loop: X = queue offset $21. At $31 or more it runs the main-thread flush ($E605) and tries again; below that, no pending row ($A5 = 0) returns; $A5 = 2 (the second tile row of a background row still to go) with offset under $0E takes the direct-row path at $E619 and returns; any other pending value ($A5 = 1 or 3, or 2 with a fuller queue) flushes and tries again. With rendering on the flush does nothing, so the loop is a spin waiting for the NMI to drain (the ~12,600 hits per room exit); with rendering off it drains the queue itself. Callers: $E5DD, $E6B0 and the room-scroll bands ($F001, $F186, $F204).

bank7.code_E66A · file 0x1E67A-0x1E6AA · 49 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E66A-$E69A

Entry $E66A (slot $C15C; also called by the flight loops) runs the scroll step $E530, then compares the 16-bit speed $B9:$B8 with the target $BB:$BA: smaller -> +2, larger -> -2, equal -> unchanged (an unsigned compare; the 'at once' bit of a speed command is applied by the command, not here). Does the next stream record fire on this row? code confirmed

bank7.code_E6CE · file 0x1E6DE-0x1E6FA · 29 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E6CE-$E6EA

Compares the row counter $B0/$B1 with the record's trigger $B2/$B3. Equal -> $E6EB. Different -> if Y is not 0 add Y to the record pointer $BC/$BD (this is how a handler that has consumed Y bytes moves on: the handlers JMP back here from $E8A8) and go on to build the row from the tables at $E8AB. Entered with Y = 0 from above, so the pointer does not move on that path.

$E6CE  A5 B0 C5 B2 D0 06 A5 B1 C5 B3 F0 11 98 F0 0B 18  |................|
$E6DE  65 BC 85 BC A5 BD 69 00 85 BD 4C AB E8           |e.....i...L..|

Fire a stream record: fetch the command and dispatch code confirmed

bank7.code_E6EB · file 0x1E6FB-0x1E702 · 8 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E6EB-$E6F2

A = command byte at ($BC),Y, Y + 1 -> $16 (the operand offset), then JSR $D6AE: the inline jump table that follows ($E6F3, ten two-byte entries, commands 0-9). The records are described under bank 4 in the screen-data notes.

$E6EB  B1 BC C8 84 16 20 AE D6                          |..... ..|

Screen-data command dispatch pointer_table confirmed

bank7.tbl_E6F3 · file 0x1E703-0x1E716 · 20 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E6F3-$E706

Ten entries, $E6F3-$E706, each holding a handler address minus one - the inline jump table convention of $D6B0, which pushes the two bytes as a fake return address and lets RTS add the one back. $E6EB reads a command byte through ($BC), the walking pointer into the bank 4 block, and hands it unmasked to $D6AE, so the command set is exactly $00-$09. THE COUNT IS BOUNDED BY STRUCTURE, not by the code: the tenth entry ends at $E706 and handler 0 begins at $E707, so the table stops exactly where its own first target starts. Reading an eleventh entry gives $16A5, which is RAM. Handlers: $E707, $E721, $E733, $E760, $E78A, $E79F, $E7B6, $E85E, $E881, $E893. All ten are now traced. WHAT EACH ONE DOES is not established.

$E6F3  06 E7 20 E7 32 E7 5F E7 89 E7 9E E7 B5 E7 5D E8  |.. .2._.......].|
$E703  80 E8 92 E8                                      |....|

Stream command 0: set the scroll speed target code confirmed

bank7.code_E707 · file 0x1E717-0x1E730 · 26 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E707-$E720

Two operand bytes -> the target $BA/$BB (the high byte's bit 7 is a flag, not part of the value). With bit 7 clear the speed ramps toward it by 2/256 a frame ($E66A). With bit 7 set the value (high byte AND $7F) is also copied into the current speed $B8/$B9 at once. RUN: the flight block and corridors 3 and 15 fire it at frame 18 and 382 of a corridor start (the flight's, then the corridor's own). Every handler ends by jumping to $E89D, which skips its last operand byte, reads the NEXT record's trigger into $B2/$B3 and returns to $E6CE with Y = the bytes consumed.

$E707  A4 16 B1 BC 85 BA C8 B1 BC 85 BB 10 0A 29 7F 85  |.............)..|
$E717  BB 85 B9 A5 BA 85 B8 4C 9D E8                    |.......L..|

Stream command 1: choose the background row table code confirmed

bank7.code_E721 · file 0x1E731-0x1E742 · 18 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E721-$E732

Two operand bytes -> the row table pointer $B4/$B5 ([rows lo][rows hi][count]) and the row cycle $A1 = 0, so the strip starts again from its first row. Every handler ends by jumping to $E89D, which skips its last operand byte, reads the NEXT record's trigger into $B2/$B3 and returns to $E6CE with Y = the bytes consumed.

$E721  A4 16 B1 BC 85 B4 C8 B1 BC 85 B5 A9 00 85 A1 4C  |...............L|

Stream command 2: start or stop enemy waves code confirmed

bank7.code_E733 · file 0x1E743-0x1E76F · 45 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E733-$E75F

One operand byte: its low two bits replace the low two bits of $60 (bit 1 = a spawn script is running; bit 0 is written with it and tested by nothing). If bit 1 is set two more operand bytes follow: the script pointer -> $66/$67, the wait counters $68 = $69 = 1 and the script bank $7F = 4 - a spawn script that lives in bank 4 and is run by the same interpreter ($F78F) as the labyrinth's, its waits counted in rows (the $A0 bit 2 flag). RUN: fired at frame 19 and 382 in corridors 3 and 15. Every handler ends by jumping to $E89D, which skips its last operand byte, reads the NEXT record's trigger into $B2/$B3 and returns to $E6CE with Y = the bytes consumed.

$E733  A4 16 B1 BC 29 03 85 10 A5 60 29 FC 05 10 85 60  |....)....`)....`|

$E753  A9 01 85 68 85 69 A9 04 85 7F 4C 9D E8           |...h.i....L..|

Stream command 3: jump to another block (the loop) code confirmed

bank7.code_E760 · file 0x1E770-0x1E799 · 42 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E760-$E789

Two operand bytes = a block address. The block's first two bytes (its first trigger) are copied into BOTH the next-trigger $B2/$B3 and the row counter $B0/$B1, the record pointer $BC/$BD is set to block + 2, and it re-enters the trigger test at $E6CC. So a jump also REBASES THE STAGE CLOCK to the target's first trigger and the target's first record fires on this very row. The launch flight's block ends with it: it fired at frames 186, 236, 287 and 338 of a corridor start (every ~50 frames: the flight's overlay pattern loops). Command 9 enters this code at $E762 when its condition holds.

$E760  A4 16 B1 BC 85 18 C8 B1 BC 85 19 A0 00 B1 18 85  |................|

Stream command 5: the boss phase code confirmed

bank7.code_E79F · file 0x1E7AF-0x1E7C5 · 23 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E79F-$E7B5

Sets $70 = 1 (the boss routine in bank 3 $917F starts the fight). In corridor 21 ($51 = $15) one operand byte follows and becomes the boss type $72; in every other corridor there is no operand (the DEY cancels the Y that the tail adds). The record is therefore one byte longer in corridor 21 only. Stream command 6: place a structure in an overlay slot code confirmed

bank7.code_E7B6 · file 0x1E7C6-0x1E86D · 168 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E7B6-$E85D

Operand 1 = the number of placements (0 = none). Each placement: a flag byte (low three bits = the slot 0-7 -> X; bit 7 no delay and no gap; bit 6 no step byte; bit 5 a single showing; bit 4 the structure is given inline), then in order: [delay -> $0430,X] and [gap -> $0408,X] unless bit 7 (bit 5 set stores $20 as the gap, which is never used because it also means one repeat), [step -> $0438,X] unless bit 6 (else 0), [repeats -> $0410,X] unless bit 5 (else 1; 0 = for ever), the start column -> $0400,X, then either [pointer lo][pointer hi][width][height] inline (bit 4: -> $0440/$0448, $0420, $0418) or a pointer to a structure [width][height][rows] (the pointer + 2 -> $0440/$0448). $EA79 then loads the working pointer $0450/$0458 and the row counter $0428 from them. The table in tools/tgl/corridor.py ('placements') reads the same format and is what the stage pictures use. Every handler ends by jumping to $E89D, which skips its last operand byte, reads the NEXT record's trigger into $B2/$B3 and returns to $E6CE with Y = the bytes consumed.

$E7B6  A4 16 B1 BC D0 03 4C 9D E8 85 14 C8 B1 BC 85 15  |......L.........|
$E7C6  C8 29 07 AA 24 15 10 07 A9 00 9D 30 04 F0 0F B1  |.)..$......0....|
$E7D6  BC C8 9D 30 04 A5 15 29 20 D0 03 B1 BC C8 9D 08  |...0...) .......|
$E7E6  04 24 15 50 04 A9 00 F0 03 B1 BC C8 9D 38 04 A5  |.$.P.........8..|
$E7F6  15 29 20 F0 04 A9 01 D0 03 B1 BC C8 9D 10 04 B1  |.) .............|
$E806  BC 9D 00 04 C8 A5 15 29 10 F0 1A B1 BC 9D 40 04  |.......)......@.|
$E816  C8 B1 BC 9D 48 04 C8 B1 BC 9D 20 04 C8 B1 BC 9D  |....H..... .....|
$E826  18 04 4C 54 E8 B1 BC 85 18 C8 B1 BC 85 19 84 16  |..LT............|

First 128 of 168 bytes. The region's SHA-1 c22093a0c4366f277d78cc7c458ea0f2bc556d44 covers all of them.

Stream command 7: load graphics for the stage code confirmed

bank7.code_E85E · file 0x1E86E-0x1E890 · 35 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E85E-$E880

While the art-load stream engine is busy ($27 non-zero) it runs a step of it ($E99F) and waits for the decompressor ($D7C3), then loops. Then two operand bytes -> the stream address $28/$29, $27 = $80 (start) and the stream's bank $7E = 4. The records are run by $E995/$E99F one step per row. Used 5 rows before a boss to swap the art (the squares before a boss). Every handler ends by jumping to $E89D, which skips its last operand byte, reads the NEXT record's trigger into $B2/$B3 and returns to $E6CE with Y = the bytes consumed.

$E85E  A5 27 F0 09 20 9F E9 20 C3 D7 4C 5E E8 A4 16 B1  |.'.. .. ..L^....|

bank7.code_E893 · file 0x1E8A3-0x1E8AC · 10 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E893-$E89C

If $70 is non-zero (a command 5 has run) it enters the jump code at $E762; otherwise it skips its two operand bytes (the INY here and the one in the tail). The corridor blocks use it as the end of a loop: the stage repeats until the boss is called, then falls through into the ending records.

Record tail: read the next trigger and go back to the test code confirmed

bank7.code_E89D · file 0x1E8AD-0x1E8BA · 14 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E89D-$E8AA

INY over the last operand byte, the next record's trigger -> $B2/$B3 (low, high), INY, and JMP $E6CE with Y = the bytes this record used. $E6CE then fires the next record on the same row if its trigger is the same, or adds Y to the record pointer and builds the row.

$E89D  C8 B1 BC 85 B2 C8 B1 BC 85 B3 C8 4C CE E6        |...........L..|

Build a row: take the next 16 ids from the strip code confirmed

bank7.code_E8AB · file 0x1E8BB-0x1E8F0 · 54 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E8AB-$E8E0

$A1 (the row cycle) is compared with the row table's count byte; at or over it wraps to 0; it is then incremented. The 16 ids are at (table pointer) + 16 x the old $A1 (computed by shifting left four times into $19), and are copied into $04C0-$04CF: the background row, before any overlay is drawn over it.

$E8AB  A0 02 A5 A1 D1 B4 90 04 A9 00 85 A1 E6 A1 A0 00  |................|
$E8BB  84 19 0A 0A 0A 26 19 0A 26 19 A0 00 71 B4 85 18  |.....&..&...q...|
$E8CB  C8 B1 B4 65 19 85 19 A0 00 A2 00 B1 18 9D C0 04  |...e............|
$E8DB  C8 E8 E0 10 D0 F5                                |......|

Build a row: draw the eight overlay slots over it code confirmed

bank7.code_E8E1 · file 0x1E8F1-0x1E983 · 147 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E8E1-$E973

For each slot X = 0-7 ($15): column $0400,X = $80 is a free slot and is skipped. A running delay $0430,X is decremented and the slot skipped. Otherwise one ROW of the structure is drawn: the working pointer $0450/$0458 is read with the codes $00 n (skip n columns: the column counter moves on), $01 (end the row early) and any other value = a metatile id written to $04C0 + column when the column is under 16, until `width` ($0420) ids have been written; the pointer is saved for the next row. When the height $0428 runs out ($0428 counts down once per row): if the repeat counter $0410 is non-zero it is decremented and a result of 0 frees the slot ($0400 = $80); otherwise the column moves by the step $0438, and a column in $20-$EF (off either edge) frees the slot, else $EA73 reloads the gap as the next delay and $EA79 resets the pointer and height - the structure starts again. This is what tools/tgl/corridor.py's simulation does, and it matched a corridor-15 savestate cell for cell (settled rows).

$E8E1  A2 00 86 15 BD 00 04 C9 80 D0 03 4C 69 E9 BD 30  |...........Li..0|
$E8F1  04 F0 06 DE 30 04 4C 69 E9 BD 50 04 85 18 BD 58  |....0.Li..P....X|
$E901  04 85 19 A0 00 BD 20 04 85 14 BD 00 04 AA B1 18  |...... .........|
$E911  D0 0A C8 8A 18 71 18 AA C8 4C 0F E9 C8 C9 01 F0  |.....q...L......|
$E921  0C E0 10 B0 03 9D C0 04 E8 C6 14 D0 E1 A6 15 98  |................|
$E931  18 65 18 9D 50 04 A5 19 69 00 9D 58 04 DE 28 04  |.e..P...i..X..(.|
$E941  D0 26 BD 10 04 F0 0C DE 10 04 D0 07 A9 80 9D 00  |.&..............|
$E951  04 D0 15 BD 00 04 18 7D 38 04 9D 00 04 C9 20 90  |.......}8..... .|

First 128 of 147 bytes. The region's SHA-1 249d66deb39df0d82eb9862ed793e38d2c7d9ad3 covers all of them.

bank7.code_E995 · file 0x1E9A5-0x1E9AE · 10 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E995-$E99E

Entered by JMP from $E6A9 (no row due) and falling in from $E994 (a row was built) with the caller's bank on the stack: one step of the art-load stream ($E99F), one step of the spawn script interpreter ($F78F), then PLA and JMP $D705 to switch back.

Art-load stream: start the next record code confirmed

bank7.code_E99F · file 0x1E9AF-0x1EA30 · 130 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $E99F-$EA20

$27 = 0 means idle (return). Otherwise it selects the stream's bank $7E. A positive $27 is a record already being decompressed (-> $EA21); bit 7 set = start the record at index $27 AND $7F: wait while the decompressor is busy ($0B), read the slot (byte 0 AND $0F), and if that slot ($0480,slot) already holds a block, EVICT it - clear the slot and turn every live object (slots 5-23) that uses the evicted block into type $28, the dissolving square. Then the record: byte 0 = flags | slot, byte 1 = block (0 = none: a 2-byte record that only evicts; bit 7 of byte 0 ends the stream), byte 2 = the first tile; PPU address = tile x 16 -> $18/$19 and the block is armed for decompression ($EA58); $27 counts on.

$E99F  A5 27 D0 01 60 48 A5 7E 20 05 D7 68 30 03 4C 21  |.'..`H.~ ..h0.L!|
$E9AF  EA A4 0B F0 03 4C 4C EA 29 7F 85 27 A8 B1 28 29  |.....LL.)..'..()|
$E9BF  7F 29 0F A8 B9 80 04 85 17 F0 1F A9 00 99 80 04  |.)..............|
$E9CF  A2 05 BD 00 05 F0 0E 20 4C CF F0 09 C5 17 D0 05  |....... L.......|
$E9DF  A9 28 9D 00 05 E8 E0 18 D0 E8 A4 27 B1 28 85 10  |.(.........'.(..|
$E9EF  C8 B1 28 85 17 D0 0D 88 A5 10 30 4C 98 18 69 82  |..(.......0L..i.|
$E9FF  85 27 D0 49 C8 A9 00 85 19 B1 28 0A 26 19 0A 26  |.'.I......(.&..&|
$EA0F  19 0A 26 19 0A 26 19 85 18 A5 17 20 58 EA E6 27  |..&..&..... X..'|

First 128 of 130 bytes. The region's SHA-1 ac91f58e70d4b0803d73fb65f956d470a0189a2d covers all of them.

Art-load stream: finish the record code confirmed

bank7.code_EA21 · file 0x1EA31-0x1EA5B · 43 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $EA21-$EA4B

Once the decompressor is free ($0B = 0) the record is entered in the slot tables: $0480,slot = the block, $0490,slot = the first tile; $27 moves to the next record, or to 0 (and returns) after a record with bit 7 set. So the tables only change after the tiles are in place.

$EA21  A5 0B D0 27 A5 27 A8 88 B1 28 85 17 29 0F AA C8  |...'.'...(..)...|

bank7.code_EA4C · file 0x1EA5C-0x1EA67 · 12 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $EA4C-$EA57

Allocate an object slot (5-23), reclaiming one if none is free code confirmed

bank7.code_EA8C · file 0x1EA9C-0x1EADB · 64 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $EA8C-$EACB

Slot $C027; the spawn-script interpreter calls it. Scans from slot 23 DOWN to 5 for a free one (type 0): returns it in Y with carry clear, so the highest free slot is used first. If none is free it reclaims, in order: the LOWEST slot whose type (AND $7F) is $0B, $0C or $0D; failing that the highest slot with type bit 7 set and flag bit 1 clear in $0578; the reclaimed object is removed ($CCBF) and its slot returned with carry clear. If nothing can be reclaimed it returns carry set (Y = 4). RUN with all slots full of type $10: carry set; a $0B in slot 12 and a $0D in 15 reclaimed 12; bit-7 types in 8 and 14 reclaimed 14; a bit-7 type with flag bit 1 set was left alone; X preserved. What types $0B-$0D are was not looked up.

$EA8C  A0 17 B9 00 05 F0 37 88 C0 04 D0 F6 C8 B9 00 05  |......7.........|
$EA9C  29 7F C9 0B F0 1F C9 0C F0 1B C9 0D F0 17 C0 17  |)...............|
$EAAC  D0 EA B9 00 05 10 07 B9 78 05 29 02 F0 07 88 C0  |........x.).....|
$EABC  04 D0 EF 38 60 8A 48 98 AA 20 BF CC 68 AA 18 60  |...8`.H.. ..h..`|

Row address: the background row's PPU address and attribute offset code confirmed

bank7.code_EACC · file 0x1EADC-0x1EAF1 · 22 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $EACC-$EAE1

From the row index $AE (0-14): the attribute offset $AA = (AE AND 14) x 4 + $C0 (low byte of the attribute address), and $AC/$AD = the row's PPU address from the pair table at $EAE2 (one entry per 64 bytes, $2000 ... $23C0; the band's second tile row is +$20). The caller adds 8 to $AD for the $2800 alias.

$EACC  A5 AE 0A A8 29 1C 0A 09 C0 85 AA B9 E2 EA 85 AC  |....)...........|
$EADC  B9 E3 EA 85 AD 60                                |.....`|

ITS EXTENT IS STRUCTURAL, NOT STATED BY THE CODE: it runs to next read base $EAE3, because nothing else claims the bytes in between. A LEAD - the reader's index range has not been checked. Table, 4 bytes data confirmed

bank7.tbl_EB00 · file 0x1EB10-0x1EB13 · 4 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $EB00-$EB03

A table of 4 bytes at $EB00, read by LDA aby at $D8CE. THE EXTENT COMES FROM THE CODE, not from the data: the same routine reads another table based at $EB04 from $D8D4, 6 bytes away in the instruction stream, so this table runs exactly up to that base. Two bases read by unrelated code would bound nothing, which is why the reads have to be neighbours. **WHAT THE BYTES MEAN IS NOT ESTABLISHED** - this region claims the extent and the reader and stops there. Generated by tools/find-tables.py.

$EB00  FC F3 CF 3F                                      |...?|

Table, 4 bytes (structural extent) data lead

bank7.tbl_EB04 · file 0x1EB14-0x1EB17 · 4 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $EB04-$EB07

A table read by LDA aby at $D8D4, starting at the first byte of an unclaimed stretch. ITS EXTENT IS STRUCTURAL, NOT STATED BY THE CODE: it runs to the claimed byte at $EB08, because nothing else claims the bytes in between. A LEAD - the reader's index range has not been checked. Generated by tools/find-tables.py (second rule).

$EB04  03 0C 30 C0                                      |..0.|

bank7.code_EB18 · file 0x1EB28-0x1EB2C · 5 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $EB18-$EB1C

Entered by JMP from the stage step ($E663) when $A0 bit 0 is set (a room exit or a finished stage), and at the very start of a game. Far-call argument at $EC38 data confirmed

bank7.inline_EC38 · file 0x1EC48-0x1EC4A · 3 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $EC38-$EC3A

Three bytes of far-call argument following `JSR $D76F` at bank 7 $EC35 - bank number, then the target address minus one, little-endian. They select **bank 6 $8000**. The stored word is $7FFF rather than $8000 because $D790 pushes these two bytes as a fake return address and lets RTS add the one back; that is not an inference, it is what the four instructions at $D799-$D7A4 do. A far call is the one reference in this ROM that no grep for an operand will ever surface, so the target is recorded here where a reader will find it.

$EC38  06 FF 7F                                         |...|

bank7.inline_ED6E · file 0x1ED7E-0x1ED80 · 3 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $ED6E-$ED70

Three bytes of far-call argument following `JSR $D76F` at bank 7 $ED6B - bank number, then the target address minus one, little-endian. They select **bank 2 $8006**. The stored word is $8005 rather than $8006 because $D790 pushes these two bytes as a fake return address and lets RTS add the one back; that is not an inference, it is what the four instructions at $D799-$D7A4 do. A far call is the one reference in this ROM that no grep for an operand will ever surface, so the target is recorded here where a reader will find it.

$ED6E  02 05 80                                         |...|

Key doors: a second key's picture code confirmed

bank7.code_ED71 · file 0x1ED81-0x1ED9D · 29 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $ED71-$ED8D

$19:$18 = PPU $1040. Scans the exits again for the first whose key is neither 8 nor the one just built; if found, far-calls the icon builder with that key (tiles $04-$07). RUN: (3, 8, 8, 5) built key 5 at $1040; (3, 3, 8, 8), (1, 8, 8, 1) and (8, 8, 2, 8) built one icon only; (4, 5, 3, 8) built 4 at $1000 and 5 at $1040 (the third key, 3, gets no picture: at most two keys are drawn per room). CHR: with keys 3 and 5 the tiles at $1000 equalled the icon built for key 3 alone, and at $1040 the icon for key 5 alone. The live CHR of the labyrinth savestate room, which has no key door, holds a generic picture there whose high plane equals the key icons' (they share one high plane), so the ring and bracket the cell notes mention are, as an inference not checked on screen, these tiles: the generic picture when no door needs a key, the key's own picture when one does.

$ED71  68 85 10 A9 10 85 19 A9 40 85 18 A0 00 B9 58 00  |h.......@.....X.|
$ED81  29 0F C9 08 F0 0B C5 10 F0 07 20 6F D7           |)......... o.|

bank7.code_ED98 · file 0x1EDA8-0x1EDB0 · 9 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $ED98-$EDA0

Callers $EC3E, $EC84, $ECAE (after a room or corridor is drawn with the screen off). $EDA1 turns the live palette into the target and blanks the screen colours, $EDE8 switches the display back on, then JMP $EDBD fades. bank7.code_EDA1 · file 0x1EDB1-0x1EDC6 · 22 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $EDA1-$EDB6

Copies the 24 live palette bytes $0181-$0198 to the fade target $04D0-$04E7 and sets each live byte to $0F (black); $017F = 3 asks the NMI to upload both the background and the sprite palettes. The backdrop $0180 is not touched.

Fade loop code confirmed

bank7.code_EDBD · file 0x1EDCD-0x1EDE0 · 20 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $EDBD-$EDD0

$D950 steps every palette byte one notch toward its target: a byte already equal is left; a byte at $0F jumps to the target's hue at its darkest ($0X); otherwise the brightness (high nibble) goes up $10 if the hue matches and the byte is below the target, and down $10 in every other case (so a different hue fades out first), a borrow giving $0F. $15 counts the bytes that moved and $017F = 3 asks for the upload. When nothing moved the loop returns. With the NMI enabled ($FF bit 7) it waits 2 frames between steps ($FFD1); with the NMI off it does all the steps at once with no waiting. So a colour at brightness 3 takes four steps to black and a fade lasts 8 frames.

$EDBD  20 50 D9 A5 15 D0 01 60 A5 FF 10 F4 A0 02 20 D1  | P.....`...... .|
$EDCD  FF 4C BD ED                                      |.L..|

Fade OUT: the screen goes to black and is switched off code confirmed

Sets the 24 target bytes $04D0-$04E7 and the backdrop $0180 to $0F, runs the fade loop, then SEI and JMP $D4C7 (rendering off: $A3 = 0 and the PPUMASK bits cleared). RUN: 5 steps at frames 0, 2, 4, 6, 8 ($30 -> $20 -> $10 -> $00 -> $0F for the first colour), returned at frame 8 with all colours $0F, $A3 = 0 and the I flag set.

$EDD1  A0 18 A9 0F 99 CF 04 88 D0 F8 8D 80 01 20 BD ED  |............. ..|

East/west scroll loop code confirmed

bank7.code_F0C2 · file 0x1F0D2-0x1F11C · 75 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $F0C2-$F10C

Waits one frame ($FFD1 with Y = 1: clear $2B and call the frame sync $DA9A Y times), then each frame: east ($A4 not 2) moves the ship's x down and adds 4 to the horizontal scroll $A8 (carrying into $A9 on the wrap); west ($A4 = 2) moves the ship up and subtracts 4 (the loop is the same shape). The ship's step is SBC #3 / ADC #3 with whatever carry the preceding LSRs left, so it ALTERNATES 3 and 4 pixels (a leftover of unused shifts). Then $E522 commits the scroll to the shadow registers and $F258 runs the ship's frame; it repeats until $A8 reaches 0 and jumps to the shared re-home at $F1DA. RUN, east exit from (y $78, x $D8): 79 frames with $A8/$A9 non-zero, $A8 = 4, 8, 12 ..., ship dx -3, -4, -3, -4 ... (220 px in all, from x 238 to 18), ending with ($A8, $A9) = (0, 1). CORRECTION: the Rendering page said the ship moves 3 px a frame; that is true of the vertical exits only.

bank7.code_F10D · file 0x1F11D-0x1F178 · 92 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $F10D-$F168

South scroll: queue a band, and re-queue its first column two calls later code confirmed

bank7.code_F204 · file 0x1F214-0x1F267 · 84 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $F204-$F257

Called once per 16-pixel band by the south arm. Waits for queue room ($E5EB), draws the band ($F03D), then stores a 4-byte record at $0400 + the cursor $0440: the PPU address $AD:$AC and the first tile of each of the two tile rows ($0130, $0150, column 0). When the cursor is 8 or more it queues the record from two calls ago as a 2-tile VERTICAL run ([$82][hi][lo][t1][t2], increment 32) and advances the cursor by 4 either way. $F20A is the second entry, which skips the draw. OBSERVED in the south exit: the flushed addresses were $2300, $2340, $2380, $2800, $2840, $2880, $28C0, $2900 ... (column 0, row pairs), the tiles $9B $9B, $9B $8D, $82 $92 ..., 14 stores and 12 flushes. WHY the south scroll needs column 0 rewritten two bands late (the scratch buffer is the class map area $0400, which is rebuilt at $F201) is a LEAD; the north arm has no such step.

A run of 15 bytes the code trace reached by following control flow, in bank 7 at $F269-$F277. **This region claims the extent and nothing else.** What the code does has not been established; the evidence is that every byte here is part of an instruction on some path the trace could follow from the interrupt vectors and the banks' entry tables, and that the trace met no illegal opcode anywhere. Corridor start: blank the screen, then choose code confirmed

bank7.code_F282 · file 0x1F292-0x1F29F · 14 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $F282-$F28F

Called by JSR $EC99 in the corridor loader ($EC8C/$EC94), which has read the corridor number from the room record, so bank 5 is live here. $EDD1 FADES the display to black: the 24 saved palette bytes $04D0-$04E7 and the backdrop $0180 are set to black and $EDBD steps the fade ($D950) every two frames until it is done (8 frames in the run), then interrupts off and rendering off ($D4C7: $A3 = 0, PPUMASK bits 3-4 cleared); $D4E3 clears the NMI-enable bit of PPUCTRL. Then: corridor 0 or 22 go to the short path at $F290; every other corridor goes on to $F2A0. Set the mode, load the launch sprites and the common scenes code confirmed

bank7.code_F2B9 · file 0x1F2C9-0x1F2E8 · 32 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $F2B9-$F2D8

$F2B9 loads A = 0 and $F2BB stores A in $0680 - slot 0's field 16, used here as the MODE: 0 = taking off (the entry from $F2B9), 1 = landing (the stage-clear code at $F6BE jumps into $F2BB with A = 1). Then $F6E5 (the launch sprite sheet), $30 |= $48 (corridor mode and bit 3), and the bank 5 scene loader $8000 twice with A = 0 and A = $20: the two common scenes 0 and 32 (the blue panel tunnel, the same for every corridor), then waits for the decompressor ($D7C3) and switches to bank 4. HARNESS LESSON: the loader calls $8000 in the LIVE bank, so bank 5 must be live (as it is when $EC8C has just read the map record); from a savestate whose bank is 6 the call lands in bank 6, far-calls the ship's handler and bumps $0680, which made every start look like mode 1.

A = $FF; only when $0680 is not 0 AND the corridor is 21 does it play sound $2D and use A = $FE. Bank 4's entry ($8000 -> $8003) takes entry number A + 2 of its pointer table: $FF -> entry 1 = $81ED (the flight block: scroll speed $0600 at once = 6 px a frame, a background row table, a palette list, three overlay records, no enemy wave), $FE -> entry 0 = $826D (a longer block: speed $0400 and then $1000). So entry 1 serves both modes and corridors 1-20; entry 0 is used only when landing from corridor 21. The parser calls these table entries 'area -2' and 'area -1'.

bank7.code_F3FC · file 0x1F40C-0x1F448 · 61 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $F3FC-$F438

bank7.tbl_F47B · file 0x1F48B-0x1F496 · 12 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $F47B-$F486

A table of 12 bytes at $F47B, read by LDA aby at $F379. THE EXTENT COMES FROM THE CODE, not from the data: the same routine reads another table based at $F487 from $F373, 6 bytes away in the instruction stream, so this table runs exactly up to that base. Two bases read by unrelated code would bound nothing, which is why the reads have to be neighbours. **WHAT THE BYTES MEAN IS NOT ESTABLISHED** - this region claims the extent and the reader and stops there. Far-call argument at $F566 data confirmed

bank7.inline_F566 · file 0x1F576-0x1F578 · 3 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $F566-$F568

Three bytes of far-call argument following `JSR $D76F` at bank 7 $F563 - bank number, then the target address minus one, little-endian. They select **bank 2 $8006**. The stored word is $8005 rather than $8006 because $D790 pushes these two bytes as a fake return address and lets RTS add the one back; that is not an inference, it is what the four instructions at $D799-$D7A4 do. A far call is the one reference in this ROM that no grep for an operand will ever surface, so the target is recorded here where a reader will find it.

$F566  02 05 80                                         |...|

bank7.inline_F5CC · file 0x1F5DC-0x1F5E8 · 13 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $F5CC-$F5D8

A queued PPU write item, written inline after `JSR $D53F` at bank 7 $F5C9. Its shape is [count][count characters]: unlike $D576 this routine takes the PPU address from $18/$19 in RAM rather than from the stream, which is what makes its block two bytes shorter. The block is 13 bytes and the caller resumes immediately after it; the length comes from the first byte by the rule in $D591 - (byte & $7F) + 3 below $C0, 9 at or above - and $D5A5 advances the routine's own pointer by exactly that. It reads " YOU GOT KEY". Characters are ASCII for the letters and space. NOT ESTABLISHED: the glyphs for codes outside A-Z and space, which only the font can settle - and no font has been found yet, because CHR is RAM on this cartridge.

Table, 13 bytes data confirmed

bank7.tbl_F772 · file 0x1F782-0x1F78E · 13 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $F772-$F77E

A table of 13 bytes at $F772, read by LDA aby at $F74C. THE EXTENT COMES FROM THE CODE, not from the data: the same routine reads another table based at $F77F from $F730, 28 bytes away in the instruction stream, so this table runs exactly up to that base. Two bases read by unrelated code would bound nothing, which is why the reads have to be neighbours. **WHAT THE BYTES MEAN IS NOT ESTABLISHED** - this region claims the extent and the reader and stops there. Generated by tools/find-tables.py.

Spawn-script interpreter: select the script's bank code confirmed

bank7.code_F78F · file 0x1F79F-0x1F7A3 · 5 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $F78F-$F793

Called from one place, $E998 in the loader/spawn entry $E995 (after the art-load step, with the caller's bank on the stack), once per stage step. $7F = the bank that holds the script: 5 for a labyrinth room's script, 4 for a corridor wave script (stream command 2 sets it). The script pointer is $66/$67 and the wait counter $68; $60 bit 1 = a script is running, bit 3 = spawning is stopped (a boss fight).

Interpreter: is it time for a tick? code confirmed

bank7.code_F794 · file 0x1F7A4-0x1F7C2 · 31 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $F794-$F7B2

No script running ($60 bit 1 clear) -> return. LABYRINTH ($30 bit 6 clear), or a corridor whose scroll speed $B8/$B9 is 0: $69 is decremented once per call and reaches 0 every 60 calls, so the script advances once a second (frames). A corridor that is scrolling advances once per NEW BACKGROUND ROW instead ($A0 bit 2, set when a row is built and cleared by the next stage step). RUN: a corridor script with no new row never advanced; with a row each call it advanced every call; a stopped corridor counted $69 like the labyrinth. RUN with synthetic scripts fed to the real routine through the harness (labyrinth mode, one call per tick).

$F794  A5 60 29 02 F0 16 24 30 50 0E A5 B8 05 B9 F0 08  |.`)...$0P.......|
$F7A4  A5 A0 29 04 D0 09 F0 04 C6 69 F0 03 4C 5C F8     |..)......i..L\.|

Interpreter: a tick - count the wait, fetch the next item code confirmed

bank7.code_F7B3 · file 0x1F7C3-0x1F7D5 · 19 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $F7B3-$F7C5

$69 = 60 for the next second, Y = 0; $68 is decremented and the script returns until it reaches 0 (so a wait of N is N ticks; a wait of 0 wraps to 255 and lasts 256 ticks - RUN). Then the next byte of the script: Y + 1 -> $15 (the offset of its operands); a value under 3 is a command, anything else a spawn (-> $F7F4). RUN with synthetic scripts fed to the real routine through the harness (labyrinth mode, one call per tick).

bank7.code_F7F4 · file 0x1F804-0x1F814 · 17 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $F7F4-$F804

Spawn item: allocate a slot code confirmed

bank7.code_F805 · file 0x1F815-0x1F82B · 23 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $F805-$F81B

A plain type takes the LOWEST free slot from 5 up ($FA5B: it only looks, never reclaims; the code switches to bank 6 around the call and back to $7F for no visible reason). A formation uses the allocator $EA8C, which takes the HIGHEST free slot and can reclaim one. If there is no slot the item is dropped exactly as when spawning is stopped (the operands are skipped, the delay honoured). RUN: the first plain spawn went to slot 5, a formation to slot 23; with every slot full both were lost and the script went on.

bank7.code_F81C · file 0x1F82C-0x1F852 · 39 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $F81C-$F842

A plain spawn stores only the type in $0500,X (the handler sets everything else on its first frame). A formation stores a TYPE-$29 SPAWNER: the formation's type in its metasprite field $0548, the count in $0560 and the interval in $07A0 (read from the two operand bytes), type $29 in $0500; the spawner (bank 6 $9A19) then makes the objects one per interval. Spawn item: the delay after it code confirmed

bank7.code_F843 · file 0x1F853-0x1F861 · 15 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $F843-$F851

The next byte is the DELAY, not a command: a value with bit 7 set is instead the next spawn item (several enemies on the same tick), handled again from $F7F4; otherwise it goes to $68 and the script returns, to act again that many ticks later. A delay of 0 is special: it does not wait, it SKIPS the following byte and carries straight on in the same tick (RUN: the byte after the zero was ignored and the next item spawned on the same tick). No bank 5 script has a zero delay; one bank 4 corridor script has (and one has a wait 0: the tail of corridor 16, see its entry).

Aim: the angle from a ratio, then the direction code confirmed

bank7.code_F8B5 · file 0x1F8C5-0x1F8DD · 25 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $F8B5-$F8CD

$F9B6 computes smaller x 256 / larger (tan of the angle off the major axis, 0-255). It is compared with the three thresholds at $F95B ($AB, $6A, $32 = tan 33.75, 22.5 and 11.25 degrees x 256, tried from the smallest) giving Y = 3 (within 11.25 degrees of the axis) down to 0 (33.75 degrees or more); Y OR the flags indexes the 32-byte table $F95E, which holds the direction 0-15. Out: A = the direction (0 right, 4 down, 8 left, 12 up, 22.5 degrees each); X unchanged; Y = the table index, NOT preserved. RUN: the 16 directions come out at every 22.5 degrees; a 910-point grid matches an independent model built from the thresholds, and 529 offsets match 'nearest of 16 by atan2' with no difference; a target on top of the shooter gives 2 (down-right); a target in slot 3 works the same.

$F8B5  A4 11 A5 10 20 B6 F9 A0 03 D9 5A F9 90 03 88 D0  |.... .....Z.....|
$F8C5  F8 98 05 38 A8 B9 5E F9 60                       |...8..^.`|

Direction to velocity: add it count times code confirmed

bank7.code_F91D · file 0x1F92D-0x1F967 · 59 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $F91D-$F957

Zeroes the object's velocity fields ($05C0/$05D8 y fraction/integer, $05F0/$0608 x) and adds the scaled vector (low six bits of $0698) times. A count of 0 returns BEFORE this, leaving the velocity as it was. So speed = 120 x scale x count / 256 pixels a frame: $0698 = $81 is 3.75, $43 5.62, $82 7.5, $83 11.25, $C1 15.0, $01 0.47, $7F 118 - the gun's speed classes. RUN, all of these.

32 bytes in four groups of eight: index = flags ($04 target above, $08 target left, $10 more horizontal than vertical) + the step Y = 0-3 above. The random number: a mix of machine state code confirmed

bank7.code_F9D1 · file 0x1F9E1-0x1F9F8 · 24 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $F9D1-$F9E8

Slot $C0EA (27 callers: banks 0, 1, 3 and 6, the demo's input timer $DAF4, the demo's corridor pick $EB53, the explosion box $FB27, $F9E9). In: nothing is required, but the result DEPENDS on A (and the carry), X, Y, the live bank and the stack depth. Out: A = the new $2A (also stored there); X and Y preserved ($10 holds X). The arithmetic, in order: A = ROL A (carry in); + $2A + carry; EOR the byte at $8000 + X (whichever bank is live: the ROM is an entropy source); + $0400,Y (RAM indexed by Y); EOR $0200 + the stack pointer (a sprite byte, indexed by how deep the stack is); + $2A + carry; - $41 (the frame counter) - borrow. So it is not a generator with a period of its own: it is stirred by the game's running state, and the DMC IRQ stirs $2A as well ($DCE8). RUN: 400 random states against a Python model of exactly this arithmetic: 0 differences (single-stepped; a frame run between would change $41 and the OAM). In 3,000 frames of play, one call per frame with A = X = Y = 0, all 256 values appeared, the top-nibble histogram was flat (162-213 per bin of 187.5 expected) and consecutive equal values occurred twice. With the machine frozen except $41 += 1 it gave 38 distinct values in 40 calls. Not established: how the sequence behaves on hardware, which has the IRQ stirring.

$F9D1  86 10 2A 65 2A 5D 00 80 79 00 04 BA 5D 00 02 A6  |..*e*]..y...]...|
$F9E1  10 65 2A E5 41 85 2A 60                          |.e*.A.*`|

Random x at the top of the screen code confirmed

bank7.code_F9E9 · file 0x1F9F9-0x1FA26 · 46 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $F9E9-$FA16

Slot $C0F0 (17 callers in banks 3 and 6: enemies that enter from the top). In: X = the object. Out: x ($0530,X) = a random value, y ($0518,X) = 8; X and Y preserved. The x comes from $F9D1: a value from $20 to $DF is used as it is; a value under $20 or $E0 and over is folded to $20 + (v AND $1F) + 2 x (v AND $3F), 32-189. Then $2A is re-stirred with the zero-page byte at (x / 2). RUN, 3,000 calls: x from 32 to 223 (191 of the 192 values seen, no value outside $20-$DF), y always 8; the folded values make the middle bins a little heavier (509 against about 375).

$F9E9  20 D1 F9 84 10 85 11 C9 20 90 04 C9 E0 90 0D 29  | ....... ......)|
$F9F9  3F 0A 85 12 A5 11 29 1F 69 20 65 12 9D 30 05 6A  |?.....).i e..0.j|
$FA09  A8 79 00 00 85 2A A9 08 9D 18 05 A4 10 60        |.y...*.......`|

Spawn an object at another object's position code confirmed

bank7.code_FA4B · file 0x1FA5B-0x1FA6A · 16 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $FA4B-$FA5A

Slot $C105 (about 25 callers in banks 1, 3 and 6, and $FE3F). In: A = the type to create, X = the object whose position is copied (the parent), Y = the slot to fill (usually just found by $FA5B). Stores the type in $0500,Y and copies the parent's x ($0530) and y ($0518) into the new slot; nothing else is written, so the slot must be a free one, whose other fields were zeroed when it was freed ($CCBF), and the child's handler sets the rest on its first frame. Out: A = the parent's y, X and Y unchanged. RUN: A = $2B, X = 9 at (111, 77), Y = 12 gave type $2B at x 111, y 77 with the other fields left at 0. The usual sequence is $FA5B (find a slot) then this, then the caller sets velocity and other fields. Boss and enemy shots use it.

$FA4B  99 00 05 BD 30 05 99 30 05 BD 18 05 99 18 05 60  |....0..0.......`|

Break a block: blast, redraw, new cell class code confirmed

bank7.code_FA96 · file 0x1FAA6-0x1FB21 · 124 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $FA96-$FB11

Entered through the slot $C117; callers are the weapon-hits-cell code (bank 6 $854B), the boss/stage routine (bank 3 $92B2, $9336) and bank 1 $8159. In: $19 = x, $18 = y of a point in the cell, $16 = the tile argument for the redraw, $15 = the cell's NEW class. It (1) allocates an object through $FA5B and makes it a type-$30 blast at the cell's centre ($19 AND $F0 | 8, $18 AND $F0 | 7); (2) adds the vertical scroll $A6 to y (wrapping at $F0), converts to a PPU address ($FA6B) and queues the new tiles through $D865 with $16; (3) restores $18/$19 and falls into the class write below. If no slot is free the blast is skipped (the BCS at $FA99 goes to $FAB7) but the tiles are still queued and the class still written. RUN on the labyrinth savestate with ($18, $19) = (100, 140) and $15 = 7: a type-$30 blast appeared at y 103, x 136, nine bytes were queued for the PPU, $18/$19 came back unchanged and the cell's class went 0 -> 7 without touching its neighbours. $FADE-$FB11, the class write, is also the jump-table slot $C11D on its own: in the labyrinth only (it does nothing when $30 bit 6 is set, a corridor, run) it stores $15 into the nibble of the class map $0400 for the cell at ($18, $19): index = (y AND $F0) / 2 | x / 32, and bit 4 of x chooses the low nibble (set) or the high one (clear).

A beam's head and tail: following its parent code lead

bank7.code_FB76 · file 0x1FB86-0x1FC36 · 177 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $FB76-$FC26

Slot $C129 (the first half). Called every frame by the Hyper Laser's run routine (weapon 9, bank 6 $8AC4) and by enemy type $22 (bank 6 $A0B8). Reads: $0668,X is the slot of a parent object and $0680,X the type that parent must keep (otherwise it returns with carry set and the caller ends the beam); $0788,X is a phase counter (the parent's x is copied into $0530,X while $0788 is below $0620,X; $0788 is set to 2 at $FBA8 and incremented at $FBC5 and $FBE4); the head is $0518,X, tested against the y velocity ($05D8,X) through $FCC3/$FCCD (beyond $D0 means off the top); $0758,X is the other end of the beam, written at $FBF7 and read by the drawing half. The branches that choose between growing, holding and ending were not worked through, so this region is a LEAD; the drawing half below was run.

A beam: a column of sprites, and its hit box code confirmed

bank7.code_FC27 · file 0x1FC37-0x1FCD2 · 156 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $FC27-$FCC2

The second half of slot $C129. Draws a vertical chain between the object's two y ends ($0518,X and $0758,X) in steps of 16 pixels, one piece per step: the object's metasprite $0548,X through $CC34, or, when that is 0, single sprites through $CF05, at x $0530,X with attributes $0560,X and base tile $06C8,X. It then sets the collision box that $D348 reads: x from $0530 - $0650 to $0530 + $0650 (clamped to 0 and 255) into $06E0/$06F8, and y from the smaller end to the larger + 15 into $0710/$0728. $0650,X is the beam's half-width. RUN as the Hyper Laser (weapon 9) from the corridor savestate: the routine was entered once per frame while the beam lived; the box half-widths were 3, 8 and 24 pixels (widths 6, 16, 48) at levels 1, 2 and 3 and the y extent ran from the beam's head (y 128 -> 68 as it rose at level 1) to a second end (147 at level 1, 191 at levels 2 and 3 in the frames sampled); level 1 drew single sprites (at x - 4), levels 2 and 3 metasprites 26 and 23.

Bank-check failure codes data confirmed

bank7.bankcheck_codes_FF3A · file 0x1FF4A-0x1FF50 · 7 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $FF3A-$FF40

Seven bytes, $0F $01 $06 $14 $0A $31 $28, one per switchable PRG bank. Read by LDA $FF3A,Y at $FF2E, the failure arm of the boot-time bank check described on the bank identity bytes, with Y holding the bank that did not answer correctly. The value is stored to $0180 and $DED2 is called, after which $FF37 spins forever. Both edges of the region are pinned by structure and not argued: the byte before is the last byte of JMP $FF37 at $FF37-$FF39, the byte after is the DEY at $FF41 that the success path branches to, and Y is bounded to 0-6 by the LDY #$06 at $FF23 and the BPL at $FF42 - so the reader accounts for exactly these seven bytes and no others. NOT ESTABLISHED: what the values mean. They are not the bank numbers and they are not consecutive. The obvious lead is that they are tile or character codes for something drawn on screen, which is a question about $DED2 and about $0180, and neither has been traced yet.

$FF3A  0F 01 06 14 0A 31 28                             |.....1(|

Far-call argument at $FF85 data confirmed

bank7.inline_FF85 · file 0x1FF95-0x1FF97 · 3 bytes · PRG bank 7 · CPU $FF85-$FF87

Three bytes of far-call argument following `JSR $D76F` at bank 7 $FF82 - bank number, then the target address minus one, little-endian. They select **bank 3 $800F**. The stored word is $800E rather than $800F because $D790 pushes these two bytes as a fake return address and lets RTS add the one back; that is not an inference, it is what the four instructions at $D799-$D7A4 do. A far call is the one reference in this ROM that no grep for an operand will ever surface, so the target is recorded here where a reader will find it.

$FF85  03 0E 80                                         |...|
